================================================================================
 HARDWARE ARCHITECTURES FOR AI - LAB 3
 Linear Regression on the scikit-learn Diabetes dataset with a Keras neuron
 Full Python execution log
================================================================================

Notebook          : linear_regression_diabetes.ipynb
Kernel run        : sequential, cells In[10] .. In[29]
Framework         : TensorFlow / Keras (functional API), optimizer SGD / Adam, loss = MSE
Dataset           : sklearn.datasets.load_diabetes()  ->  442 samples, 10 features
Accuracy metric   : coefficient of determination
                    R2 = 1 - MSE_final / Var(targets),  Var(targets) = 5929.8849  (n = 442)
                    (R2 = 1.0 perfect, R2 = 0.0 equals predicting the mean; RMSE in target units)

The five requested statements are summarised first; the complete cell-by-cell
execution log (including every training epoch) follows in section 6.


================================================================================
 1. DATASET LOADING LOG
================================================================================

    >>> from sklearn import datasets
    >>> diabetes = datasets.load_diabetes()          # In [11]

    diabetes['feature_names']
        ['age', 'sex', 'bmi', 'bp', 's1', 's2', 's3', 's4', 's5', 's6']

    Full design matrix   diabetes.data      shape = (442, 10)        # In [26]:  "shape: (442, 10)"
    Targets              diabetes.target    shape = (442,)
        quantitative measure of disease progression one year after baseline
        target mean = 152.1335   target std = 77.0057   target var = 5929.8849

    Feature selection performed in the notebook (In [12] / In [18] / In [26]):
        features     = diabetes.data[:, 2:3]   -> (442, 1)   BMI only
                       np.squeeze(features)    -> (442,)      # In [12]:  "(442, 1)" then "(442,)"
        features_2d  = diabetes.data[:, 2:4]   -> (442, 2)    BMI + blood pressure
                       # In [18]:  "features_2d shape: (442, 2)"
        features_all = diabetes.data          -> (442, 10)    all 10 features

    Batching reported by Keras during fit(): 14/14 steps  ->  ceil(442 / 32) = 14 (batch size 32).


================================================================================
 2. SINGLE NEURON MODEL DESCRIPTION
================================================================================

Topology (Keras functional API):

    inputs = Input(shape=(N,))
    output = Dense(1, activation='linear')(inputs)      # exactly ONE neuron
    model  = Model(inputs, output)
    model.compile(optimizer='sgd', loss='mse')
    model.fit(X, targets, epochs=1000)                  # batch size 32 -> 14 steps/epoch

A single linear neuron computes   y = w . x + b   (weights w, one bias b),
so the parameter count is  N inputs + 1 bias.

Three single-neuron variants were trained:

  (a) BMI only            Input(shape=(1,))   -> Dense(1, linear)     # In [13] / In [14]
      Model: "functional_2"
      +---------------------------+--------------+-----------+
      | Layer (type)              | Output Shape |   Param # |
      +---------------------------+--------------+-----------+
      | input_layer_2 (InputLayer)| (None, 1)    |         0 |
      | dense_2 (Dense)           | (None, 1)    |         2 |   (1 weight + 1 bias)
      +---------------------------+--------------+-----------+
       Total params: 2  |  Trainable params: 2  |  Non-trainable params: 0

  (b) BMI + blood pressure   Input(shape=(2,)) -> Dense(1, linear)    # In [21]
      *** this is the model saved to Lab_3_neuron.h5 (In [25]) ***
      Model: "functional_3"
      +---------------------------+--------------+-----------+
      | input_layer_3 (InputLayer)| (None, 2)    |         0 |
      | dense_3 (Dense)           | (None, 1)    |         3 |   (2 weights + 1 bias)
      +---------------------------+--------------+-----------+
       Total params: 3  |  Trainable params: 3  |  Non-trainable params: 0

  (c) all 10 features        Input(shape=(10,)) -> Dense(1, linear)   # In [26]
      Model: "functional_4"
      +---------------------------+--------------+-----------+
      | input_layer_4 (InputLayer)| (None, 10)   |         0 |
      | dense_4 (Dense)           | (None, 1)    |        11 |   (10 weights + 1 bias)
      +---------------------------+--------------+-----------+
       Total params: 11 |  Trainable params: 11 |  Non-trainable params: 0

Reference single-neuron model = (b) BMI + blood pressure, saved as Lab_3_neuron.h5.


================================================================================
 3. ACHIEVED ACCURACY  (SINGLE NEURON)
================================================================================

Training loss is MSE; final value is the loss printed at "Epoch 1000/1000".

  variant                         inputs   final MSE    RMSE      R2 (accuracy)
  ------------------------------   ------   ---------   -------   --------------
  (a) BMI only                       1     4468.9048    66.85    0.2464  (24.64 %)
  (b) BMI + blood pressure  *SAVED*  2     4016.9988    63.38    0.3226  (32.26 %)
  (c) all 10 features                10    3227.8088    56.81    0.4557  (45.57 %)

  >>> Reference single-neuron model (BMI + blood pressure, Lab_3_neuron.h5):
      final MSE = 4016.9988   RMSE = 63.38   R2 = 0.3226  (32.26 % accuracy)

  A single linear neuron explains ~25 % (1 feature) up to ~46 % (10 features) of the
  variance in disease progression - it can only fit a hyperplane, so the remaining
  error is the non-linear structure it cannot represent.


================================================================================
 4. MULTI-NEURON MODEL DESCRIPTION
================================================================================

Topology (Keras functional API):                                    # In [28]

    inputs = Input(shape=(10,))                 # all 10 features
    h      = Dense(64, activation='relu')(inputs)
    h      = Dense(32, activation='relu')(h)
    output = Dense(1,  activation='linear')(h)
    optimized_model = Model(inputs, output)
    optimized_model.compile(optimizer='adam', loss='mse')
    optimized_model.fit(features_all, targets, epochs=1000, verbose=0)

    Model: "functional_5"
    +----------------------------+--------------+-----------+
    | Layer (type)               | Output Shape |   Param # |
    +----------------------------+--------------+-----------+
    | input_layer_5 (InputLayer) | (None, 10)   |         0 |
    | dense_5 (Dense)            | (None, 64)   |       704 |   10*64 + 64
    | dense_6 (Dense)            | (None, 32)   |     2,080 |   64*32 + 32
    | dense_7 (Dense)            | (None, 1)    |        33 |   32*1  + 1
    +----------------------------+--------------+-----------+
     Total params: 2,817  |  Trainable params: 2,817  |  Non-trainable params: 0

    97 neurons total (64 + 32 + 1). Two ReLU hidden layers add non-linearity;
    the Adam optimiser adapts the per-parameter learning rate (vs. plain SGD
    for the single neuron).


================================================================================
 5. ACHIEVED ACCURACY  (MULTI-NEURON)
================================================================================

    Reported by the notebook (In [28]):   "Final loss: 2642.18"

      final MSE = 2642.18   RMSE = 51.40   R2 = 0.5544  (55.44 % accuracy)

    Comparison (all trained 1000 epochs on the same targets):

      model                                       final MSE     R2 (accuracy)
      -----------------------------------------    ---------    --------------
      single neuron - BMI only          (SGD)        4468.90    24.64 %
      single neuron - BMI + BP          (SGD)        4017.00    32.26 %
      single neuron - all 10 features   (SGD)        3227.81    45.57 %
      multi-neuron  10-64-32-1, ReLU    (Adam)       2642.18    55.44 %

    The multi-neuron network lowers MSE from ~4017 (reference single neuron) to
    ~2642 and raises R2 from ~0.32 to ~0.55: the ReLU hidden layers capture
    non-linear feature interactions that a single linear neuron cannot.

In [21]:
    # single neuron with 2 inputs (BMI + Blood Pressure)
    inputs = Input(shape=(2,))
    output = Dense(1, activation='linear')(inputs)
    
    linear_model_bmi_bp = Model(inputs, output)
    linear_model_bmi_bp.summary()
    
    linear_model_bmi_bp.compile(optimizer='sgd', loss='mse')
    
    EPOCH = 1000
    r_2d = linear_model_bmi_bp.fit(features_2d, targets, epochs=EPOCH)
Out:

In [24]:
    # visualize the learned transfer function in 3D
    fig = plt.figure()
    ax = fig.add_subplot(111, projection='3d')
    
    weight_1 = linear_model_bmi_bp.get_weights()[0][0].item()
    weight_2 = linear_model_bmi_bp.get_weights()[0][1].item()
    bias = linear_model_bmi_bp.get_weights()[1].item()
    
    x = features_2d[:, 0]
    y = features_2d[:, 1]
    z = targets
    xx = np.linspace(-0.1, 0.15, 1000)
    yy = np.linspace(-0.1, 0.1, 1000)
    
    ax.set_xlabel('BMI')
    ax.set_ylabel('Blood pressure')
    ax.set_zlabel('Disease progression')
    
    ax.scatter(x, y, z, marker='o', label='raw data')
    ax.plot(x, y, weight_1 * x + weight_2 * y + bias, 'o', color='orange', label='transfer function')
    ax.plot(xx, yy, weight_1 * xx + weight_2 * yy + bias, color='r', label='transfer function sweep')
    
    plt.legend()
Out:
    <matplotlib.legend.Legend at 0x7f52d85a1c50><Figure size 640x480 with 1 Axes>

# ## Step 11: Further Optimization — Adam Optimizer + Hidden Layers
# 
# Two improvements:
# 1. **Adam optimizer** instead of SGD — adapts the learning rate, converges faster
# 2. **Hidden layer** with ReLU — models non-linear relationships between features and target

In [28]:
    # Optimization experiment: deeper model + Adam optimizer on all 10 features
    inputs = Input(shape=(10,))
    h = Dense(64, activation='relu')(inputs)
    h = Dense(32, activation='relu')(h)
    output = Dense(1, activation='linear')(h)
    
    optimized_model = Model(inputs, output)
    optimized_model.summary()
    
    optimized_model.compile(optimizer='adam', loss='mse')
    
    EPOCH = 1000
    r_opt = optimized_model.fit(features_all, targets, epochs=EPOCH, verbose=0)
    print(f"Final loss: {r_opt.history['loss'][-1]:.2f}")
